### Task 14: Background assets

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/views/assets.go`
- Create: `internal/views/assets_test.go`

Upload is **REST-only, from the browser**: pushing megabytes of base64
through an MCP tool call to save a human from opening the UI is the wrong
trade, so the agent references an asset that already exists. The MCP
surface gets `views.list_assets` and `views.set_background` and no upload.

Bounds: 8 MB per asset, and `image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `image/webp` only.
**SVG is refused** — it is a script-execution vector when served inline,
and a raster map is what a designer has anyway.

The mime is **sniffed from the bytes**, never trusted from the caller's
`Content-Type` or the filename, and `width`/`height` are decoded from the
image rather than accepted as arguments — which is why they are the third
write-only candidate this plan names, and why step 7 reads them back
through `views.list_assets`.

- [ ] Tests: `TestAnSVGIsRefusedWhateverItCallsItself` (bytes of an SVG
  sent with `Content-Type: image/png` — the case a filename check misses);
  `TestTheMimeIsSniffedNotTrusted`; `TestAnOversizeAssetIsRefusedBeforeItIsRead`
  (the `http.MaxBytesReader` bound, so the refusal happens before eight
  megabytes are buffered); `TestWidthAndHeightAreDecodedAndReadBack`;
  `TestAnAssetIsServedWithANoSniffHeaderAndItsOwnContentType`;
  `TestDeletingAnAssetNullsTheBackgroundOfEveryViewUsingIt`;
  `TestAnAssetOfAnotherGameCannotBecomeThisViewsBackground` with its
  positive control.

- [ ] The serving route is `GET /g/{slug}/assets/{id}` registered through
  `registerContentRoute` — a GET, so viewers reach it — with
  `Content-Type` from the stored mime, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`,
  and a long `Cache-Control` because an asset's bytes never change (there
  is no update path; a new image is a new asset).

```bash
git add internal/views/assets.go internal/views/assets_test.go internal/db/queries/views.sql internal/db/dbq
git commit -m "feat: background image assets, sniffed, bounded and served"
```

### Task 15: The `views.*` MCP tools, the REST mirror and the SSE kinds

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/web/mcp_views.go`
- Create: `internal/web/api_views.go`
- Create: `internal/web/mcp_views_test.go`
- Create: `internal/web/api_views_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/web/mcp.go` (`MCPDeps.Views`, registration)
- Modify: `internal/web/mcp_errors.go` and `internal/web/api_metamodel.go` (four new arms)
- Modify: `internal/web/server.go` (the routes)
- Modify: `internal/web/publish.go` or `internal/views/events.go` (the `view.*` kinds)
- Modify: `cmd/maestro/main.go` (build the service)

Nine tools, exactly the spec's §5.5:

| Tool | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `views.list` | saved views in the game; slim rows (key, name, renderer, stale flag) |
| `views.get` | one view including its query and renderer params |
| `views.upsert` | create or replace by `(project, key)`, with `expected_version` |
| `views.remove` | delete a view, its positions and its refs |
| `views.run` | execute a saved view, **or an inline query**, and return the envelope |
| `views.validate` | compile and validate without saving and without executing |
| `views.set_positions` | write `[{entity_type, entity_key, x, y, pinned}]` |
| `views.clear_positions` | drop positions for a view, or for listed entities |
| `views.set_background` | point a view at an existing asset, with scale and offset |

Two of them carry an argument worth defending in the description.
**`views.run` accepts an inline `query` with no saved view**: "which
quests can a Mage reach?" asked once, in conversation, should not leave a
saved artefact behind in the game. **`views.validate` exists so composing
a view is a loop**: an agent writing a five-step traversal against an
unfamiliar game will get it wrong twice, and validating without executing
is fast, free of timeouts, and returns the same structured errors, so the
loop closes in seconds instead of after a five-second graph walk.

- [ ] **Registration goes through `addScopedTool`**, like every other
  tool, so `TestEveryMCPToolGoesThroughAddScopedTool` passes; **routes go
  through `registerContentRoute`** with their method named, so
  `TestEveryContentRouteIsRegisteredAsContent` and
  `TestEveryContentWriteRouteRefusesAViewer` pass — and the second of
  those is what makes this plan's open question O3 (running is viewer,
  saving is editor) true rather than intended.

- [ ] **The four new wire codes**, added to `mcpErrorFor` and
  `writeDomainError` **in the same commit as the sentinels they map**:
  `query_invalid` → 400 with `fieldDetails`, `renderer_requirements` →
  422, `limit_exceeded` → 400, `query_stale` → 409 with the diagnostics
  under `details.stale`. `statusForCode` gains the three that are not
  422 by default. The convention test:

```go
// TestEveryViewsSentinelHasAWireCode iterates views.Sentinels() rather
// than repeating a list, so a fifth sentinel added later cannot be
// forgotten on either surface.
func TestEveryViewsSentinelHasAWireCode(t *testing.T) {
	for _, sentinel := range views.Sentinels() {
		result := mcpErrorFor(context.Background(), "views.run", Caller{}, sentinel)
		code := codeOfResult(t, result)
		if code == errCodeInternal {
			t.Errorf("%v maps to internal_error: a caller-fixable failure must never do that", sentinel)
		}
	}
}
```

  and its REST twin, alongside the existing
  `TestWriteDomainErrorIsTheRESTTwinOfMCPErrorFor`.

- [ ] **`/events` gains `view.upserted` and `view.removed`** per game, so
  a designer watching a view is told it changed. Whether the UI re-runs
  automatically or shows a refresh affordance is sub-project 5's; this
  task ships the kinds and asserts they arrive, with
  `TestAViewEventReachesAViewerAndATokenAlike`.

- [ ] **The read-back sweep this task owes.** Through the MCP tools, not
  the service: upsert a view with `renderer_params`, `description`,
  `layout_mode`, `layout_seed`, `background_scale` and
  `background_offset`, then `views.get` and assert **all six** came back.
  Then `views.set_positions` and `views.get` and assert `pinned`. Then
  `views.list_assets` and assert `width`, `height` and `mime`.

- [ ] **The isolation sweep.** Every one of the nine tools called with a
  foreign game's token, each asserting `scope_violation` — and
  `views.run` twice, once with a saved key and once with an inline query,
  because they take different paths to the same execution.

```bash

        internal/web/api_metamodel.go internal/web/server.go cmd/maestro/main.go
```

### Task 16: End to end — the spec's definition of done

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/web/views_e2e_test.go`

The spec's §9, executed as one test against a running server, over MCP,
exactly as an agent would:

1. Seed a game: `class`, `quest`, `zone`; `available_to`, `requires`,
   `takes_place_in`; nine quests across two zones and three level bands.
2. Call `views.validate` with a query that names `avilable_to`. Assert
   `query_invalid` at `/traverse/0/via/0` naming the key back.
3. Call `views.validate` again with `min_level` compared by `contains`.
   Assert `query_invalid` at `/traverse/0/where/op` naming the declared
   type and the operators it does answer. **Two validations, the loop the
   tool exists for.**
4. Call `views.upsert` once, with the spec's §3.1 query, renderer
   `graph`, `color_by` the zone one hop away. Assert version 1.
5. Call `views.run` and assert the quests between level 20 and 30 come
   back, each carrying its zone name in `attrs.color_by`, and that a
   quest at level 12 does not.
6. Call `views.set_positions` for four of them, `views.run` again, and
   assert the four coordinates come back **and** that the other nodes came
   back without positions rather than at the origin.
7. Add twelve more quests through `entities.bulk_upsert`, run again, and
   assert the four positions are unchanged and the twelve new nodes
   arrived unpinned. This is the spec's "the positions survive the agent
   adding twelve more quests".
8. Rename `available_to` to `usable_by` through `relation_types.upsert`.
   Run again and assert `query_stale` by default, with a
   `relation_type_renamed` diagnostic **at `/traverse/0/via/0`** — the
   pointer is the point: the view reports which step broke instead of
   quietly drawing something false.
9. Run with `on_stale: "best_effort"` and assert a non-empty result plus
   the same diagnostic.
10. `views.upsert` the repaired query with `expected_version: 1`, assert
    version 2, and run clean.
11. Delete the `zone` entity type (after deleting its entities) and assert
    the deletion **succeeds** and its response lists this view among the
    ones it broke, with the pointer into the projection.
12. The isolation sweep: every tool with a second game's token.
13. Record `stats.duration_ms` for step 5 in the commit message. That
    number is the evidence this plan's open question O2 (indexed jsonb
    fields) is to be re-argued with, and recording it is the difference
    between deciding that with data and deciding it again by argument.

- [ ] **Then run the product by hand.** `make dev`, seed the game through
  the MCP endpoint with a real token, open the view in a browser, and
  confirm the envelope arrives. The one defect nine review rounds missed
  was found this way and no other.

```bash
git add internal/web/views_e2e_test.go
git commit -m "test: the views definition of done, end to end over MCP"
```

## Self-review notes

Checked against `2026-09-02-views-and-query-language-design.md`, section
by section:

- **§1 What this spec decides** — D2 implemented, D1 and D3 absent: no
  built-in view anywhere (the catalogue in Task 10 is renderers, not
  views), and no agent-authored renderer code (`renderer` is a name from a
  closed set, checked at upsert). The spec's own test — *"the quests a
  Mage can reach between level 20 and 30, coloured by zone"* as one saved
  view an agent composes in one call — is Task 16, steps 4 and 5.
- **§2 The query language** — the JSON document and its `v` (Task 2), the
  five stages `from`/`traverse`/`nodes`/`edges`/`project` (Tasks 2, 6, 8,
  9), `limits` (Tasks 3 and 7), `params` (Tasks 2 and 4), predicates and
  the exact operator table including `list<text>` as the whole list
  family (Task 3), attribute references with the one-hop `related` and
  its `ambiguous` flag (Task 9), and the single envelope (Task 6).
- **§3 Worked examples** — all three compile in Task 6's golden test, and
  §3.1 is Task 16 end to end. §3.3's `edge_where` on a soft-reference
  `text` field is Task 4 (resolution against the *relation type's*
  schema) and Task 8 (the edge predicate inside the walk).
- **§4 Compiling to SQL** — §4.1's resolve-before-compile is Task 4 and
  is the reason Task 6 can assert that no caller value reaches the
  statement text; §4.2's three load-bearing lines are Task 5's three reds;
  the `jsonb_typeof` guard and the `invalid` exclusion are Task 6; §4.3's
  bounds table is Tasks 3 and 7; §4.4's honest reading of the indexes is
  the migration comment in Task 1 plus this plan's open question O2.
- **§5 Renderers, layout and storage** — the catalogue and its
  requirements are Task 10; §5.2's three layout modes are stored and
  returned as a client contract (Task 13, and open question O1 says why
  the server computes nothing); §5.3's positions with both failure modes
  are Task 13; §5.4's four tables **with the composite keys the section
  itself says the DDL lacks** are Task 1; §5.5's nine tools, response-size
  discipline, isolation and error codes are Task 15 — with
  `query_timeout` deliberately not shipped and argued.
- **§6 Validation and staleness** — the id-then-key resolution order,
  both strictnesses, the eight diagnostic codes, the rename that does not
  rewrite, and the type deletion that is allowed and lists what it broke:
  Task 12, exercised end to end in Task 16 steps 8–11.
- **§7 Open questions** — O1, O2, O3, O4, O5, O7, O8 and O9 are all
  settled in "Open questions settled", each with an argument; O6 was
  already closed by the spec.
- **§8 What the existing schema needs** — the four tables and the
  `relations (relation_type_id, target_id)` index are Task 1, and the
  index carries the `EXPLAIN` evidence the metamodel plan's correction 6
  demanded before adding it. The two recorded frictions (relations have
  no key and no version; `entities_fields_idx` serves containment only)
  are named in "What in the shipped code makes this harder" and shape
  Tasks 1, 6 and 13.
- **§9 Definition of done** — Task 16, step by step, plus the hand check.

Deliberately **not** here: what any of it looks like (sub-project 5 and
`/impeccable`), cycle detection and unreachable-content reports (the
analysis sub-project), the skill bundle that teaches an agent when to
compose a view rather than list entities, and aggregation.

Three things this plan changes **outside** the views domain, named so a
reviewer looks at them first:

1. **`internal/graph` is new** (Task 5) and has one caller today. Its
   package comment says so rather than claiming the second.
2. **`internal/graph`'s API grows in Task 8** (`EdgePredicate`), which is
   a change to a package Task 5 shipped; the commit touches both.
3. **`internal/web` gains four wire codes** (Task 15) on both surfaces in
   one commit, with a convention test that iterates the sentinel list.
